```
1→"""Minimal database client for API orchestrator logging."""
     2→
     3→import os
     4→import logging
     5→from datetime import datetime, timezone
     6→from supabase import create_client, Client
     7→
     8→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
     9→
    10→
    11→class DatabaseClient:
    12→    """Minimal database client for centralized logging support."""
    13→    
    14→    def __init__(self):
    15→        """Initialize Supabase client."""
    16→        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
    17→        supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
    18→        
    19→        if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
    20→            raise ValueError("SUPABASE_URL and SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY must be set")
    21→        
    22→        self.supabase: Client = create_client(supabase_url, supabase_key)
    23→
    24→    def register_worker(self, worker_id: str) -> bool:
    25→        """Register or update an API worker in the workers table.
    26→        
    27→        Uses upsert to handle both new registration and reconnection cases.
    28→        """
    29→        try:
    30→            self.supabase.table('workers').upsert({
    31→                'id': worker_id,
    32→                'instance_type': 'api',
    33→                'status': 'active',
    34→                'last_heartbeat': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(),
    35→                'metadata': {'type': 'api_orchestrator'},
    36→                'created_at': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
    37→            }, on_conflict='id').execute()
    38→            
    39→            logger.info(f"Registered API worker: {worker_id}")
    40→            return True
    41→        except Exception as e:
    42→            logger.error(f"Failed to register API worker {worker_id}: {e}")
    43→            return False
    44→
    45→
    46→
    47→
    48→
```

```
    16→class DatabaseClient:
    17→    """Database client for orchestrator operations."""
    18→    
    19→    def __init__(self):
    20→        load_dotenv()
    21→        
    22→        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
    23→        supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
    24→        
    25→        if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
    26→            raise ValueError("SUPABASE_URL and SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY must be set")

    30→    # Task operations (minimal - most task management is done by headless workers)
    31→    async def count_available_tasks_via_edge_function(self, include_active: bool = True) -> int:
    32→        """
    33→        Get available task count using the new task-counts endpoint.
    34→        This ensures the orchestrator sees exactly the same task count as workers.
    35→        
    36→        Args:
    37→            include_active: If True, includes both Queued and In Progress tasks.
    38→                           If False, only includes Queued tasks.
    39→        
    40→        Returns:
    41→            Number of available tasks that workers can see.
    42→        """
    43→        try:
    44→            supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
    45→            supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
    46→            
    47→            if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
    48→                logger.error("Missing Supabase edge-function configuration")
    49→                return 0
    50→            
    51→            task_counts_url = f"{supabase_url}/functions/v1/task-counts"
    52→            
    53→            headers = {
    54→                "Authorization": f"Bearer {supabase_key}",
    55→                "Content-Type": "application/json"
    56→            }
    57→            
    58→            payload = {
    59→                "run_type": "gpu",
    60→                "include_active": include_active
    61→            }
    62→            
    63→            timeout = aiohttp.ClientTimeout(total=30)
    64→            async with aiohttp.ClientSession(timeout=timeout) as session:
    65→                async with session.post(task_counts_url, headers=headers, json=payload) as response:
    66→                    if response.status == 200:
    67→                        data = await response.json()
    68→                        # Extract count from new endpoint format
    69→                        if "totals" in data:
    70→                            task_count = data["totals"].get("queued_plus_active" if include_active else "queued_only", 0)
    71→                        else:
    72→                            task_count = data.get("available_tasks", 0)
    73→                        logger.debug(f"Task counts endpoint returned {task_count} available tasks (include_active={include_active})")
    74→                        return task_count
    75→                    else:
    76→                        logger.error(f"Task counts endpoint returned status {response.status}: {await response.text()}")
    77→                        return 0
    78→                        
    79→        except Exception as e:
    80→            logger.error(f"Failed to get task count: {e}")
    81→            return 0

    83→    async def get_detailed_task_counts_via_edge_function(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    84→        """
    85→        Get detailed task count breakdown using the new task-counts endpoint.
    86→        This provides comprehensive debugging information for scaling decisions.

    88→        Returns:

    90→        """
    91→        try:
    92→            supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
    93→            supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")

    95→            if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
    96→                logger.error("Missing Supabase edge-function configuration")

    98→            
    99→            task_counts_url = f"{supabase_url}/functions/v1/task-counts"
   100→            
   101→            headers = {
   102→                "Authorization": f"Bearer {supabase_key}",
   103→                "Content-Type": "application/json"
   104→            }
   105→            
   106→            payload = {
   107→                "run_type": "gpu"
   108→            }
   109→            
   110→            timeout = aiohttp.ClientTimeout(total=30)
   111→            async with aiohttp.ClientSession(timeout=timeout) as session:
   112→                async with session.post(task_counts_url, headers=headers, json=payload) as response:
   113→                    if response.status == 200:
   114→                        data = await response.json()
   115→                        logger.info(f"✅ Task counts endpoint returned detailed breakdown: {data.get('totals', {})}")
   116→                        
   117→                        # Log response for debugging
   118→                        totals = data.get('totals', {})
   119→                        logger.info(f"🔍 EDGE_FUNCTION_DEBUG Full response keys: {list(data.keys())}")
   120→                        logger.info(f"🔍 EDGE_FUNCTION_DEBUG Totals: {totals}")
   121→                        
   122→                        # Check if new breakdown fields are present (from updated edge function)
   123→                        potentially_claimable = totals.get('potentially_claimable')
   124→                        if potentially_claimable is not None:
   125→                            # New edge function with proper breakdown
   126→                            queued_only = totals.get('queued_only', 0)  # Claimable right now
   127→                            blocked_capacity = totals.get('blocked_by_capacity', 0)
   128→                            blocked_deps = totals.get('blocked_by_deps', 0)
   129→                            blocked_settings = totals.get('blocked_by_settings', 0)
   130→                            logger.info(f"✅ New edge function breakdown: queued_only={queued_only}, "
   131→                                       f"capacity_blocked={blocked_capacity}, deps_blocked={blocked_deps}, "
   132→                                       f"settings_blocked={blocked_settings}, potentially_claimable={potentially_claimable}")
   133→                        else:
   134→                            # Legacy edge function - apply workaround for backward compatibility
   135→                            logger.info("⚠️ Legacy edge function detected (no potentially_claimable field)")
   136→                            reported_queued = totals.get('queued_only', 0)
   137→                            reported_active = totals.get('active_only', 0)
   138→                            
   139→                            # Compute actual counts from arrays if available
   140→                            queued_tasks_list = data.get('queued_tasks', [])
   141→                            active_tasks_list = data.get('active_tasks', [])
   142→                            actual_queued = len(queued_tasks_list)
   143→                            actual_active = len(active_tasks_list) if active_tasks_list else reported_active
   144→                            
   145→                            # LEGACY WORKAROUND: Override if totals don't match array
   146→                            if actual_queued > 0 and reported_queued == 0:
   147→                                logger.warning(f"⚠️ LEGACY_WORKAROUND: totals.queued_only={reported_queued} but "
   148→                                             f"queued_tasks has {actual_queued} items! Overriding.")
   149→                                data['totals']['queued_only'] = actual_queued
   150→                                data['totals']['queued_plus_active'] = actual_queued + actual_active
   151→                        
   152→                        return data

   165→    # Worker operations
   166→    async def get_workers(self, status: List[str] = None) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   167→        """Get GPU workers by status using only database status field.
   168→        
   169→        Excludes API workers (instance_type='api') which are managed separately
   170→        and should not be subject to GPU worker heartbeat monitoring.
   171→        """
   172→        try:
   173→            # Order by created_at DESC to get most recent workers first
   174→            # This ensures we see active/spawning workers within the 1000 row Supabase limit
   175→            # Exclude API workers - they have their own lifecycle and don't need GPU monitoring
   176→            query = self.supabase.table('workers').select('*').neq('instance_type', 'api').order('created_at', desc=True)
   177→            
   178→            if status:
   179→                # Filter directly by database status - no mapping needed
   180→                query = query.in_('status', status)
   181→            
   182→            result = query.execute()
   183→            
   184→            # Return workers using only database status
   185→            workers = []
   186→            for worker in (result.data or []):
   187→                metadata = worker.get('metadata') or {}
   188→                
   189→                mapped_worker = {
   190→                    'id': worker['id'],
   191→                    'instance_type': worker['instance_type'],
   192→                    'status': worker['status'],  # Use database status only
   193→                    'created_at': worker['created_at'],
   194→                    'last_heartbeat': worker.get('last_heartbeat'),
   195→                    'metadata': metadata
   196→                }
   197→                workers.append(mapped_worker)
   198→            
   199→            return workers
   200→            
   201→        except Exception as e:
   202→            logger.error(f"Failed to get workers: {e}")
   203→            return []
   204→    
   205→    async def get_worker_by_id(self, worker_id: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   206→        """Get a specific worker by ID."""
   207→        try:
   208→            result = self.supabase.table('workers').select('*').eq('id', worker_id).single().execute()
   209→            
   210→            if result.data:
   211→                worker = result.data
   212→                metadata = worker.get('metadata') or {}
   213→                
   214→                return {
   215→                    'id': worker['id'],
   216→                    'instance_type': worker['instance_type'],
   217→                    'status': worker['status'],  # Use database status only
   218→                    'created_at': worker['created_at'],
   219→                    'last_heartbeat': worker.get('last_heartbeat'),
   220→                    'metadata': metadata
   221→                }
   222→            
   223→            return None
   224→            
   225→        except Exception as e:
   226→            logger.error(f"Failed to get worker {worker_id}: {e}")
   227→            return None
   228→    
   229→    async def create_worker_record(self, worker_id: str, instance_type: str, runpod_id: str = None) -> bool:
   230→        """Create a new worker record (optimistic registration)."""
   231→        try:
   232→            metadata = {'orchestrator_status': 'spawning'}
   233→            if runpod_id:
   234→                metadata['runpod_id'] = runpod_id
   235→            
   236→            self.supabase.table('workers').insert({
   237→                'id': worker_id,
   238→                'instance_type': instance_type,
   239→                'status': 'inactive',  # DB status
   240→                'metadata': metadata,
   241→                'created_at': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
   242→            }).execute()
   243→            
   244→            logger.debug(f"Created worker record: {worker_id}")
   245→            return True
   246→            
   247→        except Exception as e:
   248→            logger.error(f"Failed to create worker record {worker_id}: {e}")
   249→            return False
   250→    
   251→    async def update_worker_status(self, worker_id: str, status: str, metadata_update: Dict[str, Any] = None) -> bool:
   252→        """Update worker status and metadata."""
   253→        try:
   254→            # Get current metadata directly from database
   255→            try:
   256→                result = self.supabase.table('workers').select('metadata').eq('id', worker_id).single().execute()
   257→                metadata = result.data.get('metadata') or {} if result.data else {}
   258→            except Exception:
   259→                metadata = {}
   260→            
   261→            # Mirror the main status into orchestrator_status for consistency
   262→            metadata['orchestrator_status'] = status
   263→
   264→            # Merge in any additional metadata (caller wins)
   265→            if metadata_update:
   266→                metadata.update(metadata_update)
   267→            
   268→            # Update in database with the provided status directly
   269→            result = self.supabase.table('workers').update({
   270→                'status': status,  # Use status directly, no mapping
   271→                'metadata': metadata
   272→            }).eq('id', worker_id).execute()
   273→            
   274→            logger.debug(f"Updated worker {worker_id} status to {status}")
   275→            return True
   276→            
   277→        except Exception as e:
   278→            logger.error(f"Failed to update worker {worker_id} status: {e}")
   279→            return False
   280→    
   281→    async def mark_worker_error(self, worker_id: str, reason: str) -> bool:
   282→        """Mark a worker as error with reason."""
   283→        try:
   284→            metadata_update = {
   285→                'error_reason': reason,
   286→                'error_time': datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat()
   287→            }
   288→            
   289→            return await self.update_worker_status(worker_id, 'error', metadata_update)
   290→            
   291→        except Exception as e:
   292→            logger.error(f"Failed to mark worker {worker_id} as error: {e}")
   293→            return False
   294→    
   295→    async def update_worker_heartbeat(self, worker_id: str, vram_total_mb: int = None, vram_used_mb: int = None) -> bool:
   296→        """Update worker heartbeat and optionally VRAM metrics."""
   297→        try:
   298→            params = {'worker_id_param': worker_id}

   300→            if vram_total_mb is not None:
   301→                params['vram_total_mb_param'] = vram_total_mb
   302→                params['vram_used_mb_param'] = vram_used_mb or 0
   303→            
   304→            self.supabase.rpc('func_update_worker_heartbeat', params).execute()

   308→            
   309→        except Exception as e:

   313→    # Helper functions
   314→    async def has_running_tasks(self, worker_id: str) -> bool:
   315→        """Check if a worker has any running tasks."""
   316→        try:
   317→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('id').eq('worker_id', worker_id).eq('status', 'In Progress').execute()
   318→            
   319→            return len(result.data or []) > 0
   320→            
   321→        except Exception as e:
   322→            logger.error(f"Failed to check running tasks for worker {worker_id}: {e}")

   325→    async def has_worker_ever_claimed_task(self, worker_id: str) -> bool:
   326→        """Check if a worker has ever been assigned any task (any status).
   327→        
   328→        Used to detect workers that started but never successfully claimed work,
   329→        indicating potential GPU initialization failures or other startup issues.
   330→        """
   331→        try:
   332→            # Check if ANY task has ever been assigned to this worker
   333→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('id').eq('worker_id', worker_id).limit(1).execute()
   334→            
   335→            return len(result.data or []) > 0
   336→            
   337→        except Exception as e:
   338→            logger.error(f"Failed to check if worker {worker_id} ever claimed tasks: {e}")
   339→            # Return True on error to avoid false terminations
   340→            return True
   341→    
   342→    async def get_running_tasks_for_worker(self, worker_id: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   343→        """Get all running tasks for a worker."""
   344→        try:
   345→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('*').eq('worker_id', worker_id).eq('status', 'In Progress').execute()
   346→            
   347→            # Map to expected format
   348→            tasks = []
   349→            for task in (result.data or []):
   350→                mapped_task = {
   351→                    'id': task['id'],
   352→                    'status': 'Running',  # Map back to orchestrator status
   353→                    'generation_started_at': task.get('generation_started_at'),
   354→                    'task_type': task.get('task_type')
   355→                }
   356→                tasks.append(mapped_task)
   357→            
   358→            return tasks
   359→            
   360→        except Exception as e:
   361→            logger.error(f"Failed to get running tasks for worker {worker_id}: {e}")
   362→            return []

   364→    @staticmethod
   365→    def _build_task_reset_payload(error_message: str, attempts: Optional[int] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   366→        """Build a consistent payload for task reset updates."""
   367→        payload: Dict[str, Any] = {
   368→            'status': 'Queued',
   369→            'worker_id': None,
   370→            'generation_started_at': None,
   371→            'generation_processed_at': None,
   372→            'error_message': error_message,
   373→        }
   374→        if attempts is not None:
   375→            payload['attempts'] = attempts
   376→        return payload
   377→    
   378→    async def reset_orphaned_tasks(self, failed_worker_ids: List[str]) -> int:
   379→        """Reset tasks from failed workers back to queued status (includes orchestrator tasks with assigned workers)."""
   380→        try:
   381→            if not failed_worker_ids:
   382→                return 0
   383→            
   384→            # Find all tasks from these workers that need to be reset
   385→            # Since we're explicitly looking for tasks assigned to failed workers,
   386→            # we include orchestrator tasks - if they have a worker_id, they should be reset
   387→            tasks_result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('id, task_type').eq('status', 'In Progress').in_('worker_id', failed_worker_ids).lt('attempts', 3).execute()
   388→            
   389→            if not tasks_result.data:
   390→                if len(failed_worker_ids) <= 5:
   391→                    logger.debug(f"No orphaned tasks found for workers: {failed_worker_ids}")
   392→                else:
   393→                    logger.debug(f"No orphaned tasks found for {len(failed_worker_ids)} workers")
   394→                return 0
   395→            
   396→            # Reset all orphaned tasks (including orchestrator tasks)
   397→            task_ids = [task['id'] for task in tasks_result.data]
   398→            orchestrator_task_count = sum(1 for task in tasks_result.data if '_orchestrator' in task.get('task_type', '').lower())
   399→            
   400→            reset_result = self.supabase.table('tasks').update(
   401→                self._build_task_reset_payload('Reset - orphaned from failed worker')
   402→            ).in_('id', task_ids).execute()
   403→            
   404→            count = len(reset_result.data) if reset_result.data else 0
   405→            
   406→            # Logging
   407→            if count > 0:
   408→                if len(failed_worker_ids) <= 5:
   409→                    log_msg = f"Reset {count} orphaned tasks from workers: {failed_worker_ids}"
   410→                else:
   411→                    log_msg = f"Reset {count} orphaned tasks from {len(failed_worker_ids)} workers"
   412→                
   413→                if orchestrator_task_count > 0:
   414→                    log_msg += f" (including {orchestrator_task_count} orchestrator tasks)"
   415→                
   416→                logger.info(log_msg)
   417→            elif failed_worker_ids:
   418→                # If we checked workers but found no orphaned tasks, log at debug level only
   419→                if len(failed_worker_ids) <= 5:
   420→                    logger.debug(f"Checked {len(failed_worker_ids)} workers for orphaned tasks: {failed_worker_ids}")
   421→                else:
   422→                    logger.debug(f"Checked {len(failed_worker_ids)} workers for orphaned tasks (list truncated)")
   423→            
   424→            return count
   425→            
   426→        except Exception as e:
   427→            logger.error(f"Failed to reset orphaned tasks: {e}")
   428→            return 0
   429→    
   430→    async def reset_unassigned_orphaned_tasks(self, timeout_minutes: int = 15) -> int:
   431→        """Reset tasks stuck in 'In Progress' with no worker_id assigned (excludes orchestrator tasks)."""
   432→        try:
   433→            # Find tasks that are in progress but have no worker assigned
   434→            # and have been stuck for longer than the timeout
   435→            # EXCLUDE orchestrator tasks since they can legitimately run for extended periods
   436→            cutoff_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(minutes=timeout_minutes)
   437→            
   438→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('id, task_type').eq('status', 'In Progress').is_('worker_id', 'null').lt('generation_started_at', cutoff_time.isoformat()).execute()
   439→            
   440→            if not result.data:
   441→                return 0
   442→            
   443→            # Filter out orchestrator tasks
   444→            non_orchestrator_tasks = [
   445→                task for task in result.data 
   446→                if '_orchestrator' not in task.get('task_type', '').lower()
   447→            ]
   448→            
   449→            if not non_orchestrator_tasks:
   450→                logger.debug(f"Found {len(result.data)} unassigned tasks, but all are orchestrator tasks - skipping reset")
   451→                return 0
   452→            
   453→            task_ids = [task['id'] for task in non_orchestrator_tasks]
   454→            
   455→            # Reset these tasks back to Queued status (only if attempts < 3)
   456→            reset_result = self.supabase.table('tasks').update(
   457→                self._build_task_reset_payload('Reset - stuck in progress with no worker assigned')
   458→            ).in_('id', task_ids).lt('attempts', 3).execute()
   459→            
   460→            count = len(reset_result.data) if reset_result.data else 0
   461→            
   462→            if count > 0:
   463→                logger.warning(f"Reset {count} unassigned orphaned tasks that were stuck in progress (excluded orchestrator tasks)")
   464→                # Log task IDs for debugging
   465→                if count <= 5:
   466→                    logger.warning(f"Reset unassigned orphaned task IDs: {task_ids}")
   467→                else:
   468→                    logger.warning(f"Reset {count} unassigned orphaned tasks (IDs truncated)")
   469→            
   470→            return count
   471→            
   472→        except Exception as e:
   473→            logger.error(f"Failed to reset unassigned orphaned tasks: {e}")
   474→            return 0
   475→    
   476→    async def reset_api_worker_orphaned_tasks(self, timeout_minutes: int = 5) -> int:
   477→        """Reset tasks stuck in 'In Progress' assigned to API workers (api-worker-*).
   478→        
   479→        API workers (like api-worker-main) are not tracked in the workers table,
   480→        so when the API orchestrator crashes/restarts, their in-flight tasks
   481→        become orphaned with no cleanup mechanism.
   482→        """
   483→        try:
   484→            cutoff_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(minutes=timeout_minutes)
   485→            
   486→            # Find tasks that are:
   487→            # - In Progress
   488→            # - Assigned to an api-worker-* (not a GPU worker)
   489→            # - Have been stuck for longer than the timeout
   490→            # - Have attempts < 3 (allow retry)
   491→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('id, task_type, worker_id, attempts').eq('status', 'In Progress').like('worker_id', 'api-worker-%').lt('generation_started_at', cutoff_time.isoformat()).lt('attempts', 3).execute()
   492→            
   493→            if not result.data:
   494→                return 0
   495→            
   496→            # Reset each task individually to properly increment attempts
   497→            count = 0
   498→            task_ids = []
   499→            for task in result.data:
   500→                task_id = task['id']
   501→                current_attempts = task.get('attempts', 0)
   502→                new_attempts = current_attempts + 1
   503→                
   504→                try:
   505→                    reset_result = self.supabase.table('tasks').update(
   506→                        self._build_task_reset_payload(
   507→                            f'Reset - orphaned from API worker timeout (attempt {new_attempts}/3)',
   508→                            attempts=new_attempts,
   509→                        )
   510→                    ).eq('id', task_id).execute()
   511→                    
   512→                    if reset_result.data:
   513→                        count += 1
   514→                        task_ids.append(task_id)
   515→                except Exception as e:
   516→                    logger.error(f"Failed to reset orphaned task {task_id}: {e}")
   517→            
   518→            if count > 0:
   519→                logger.warning(f"Reset {count} orphaned API worker tasks stuck in progress for >{timeout_minutes}m")
   520→                if count <= 5:
   521→                    logger.warning(f"Reset API orphaned task IDs: {task_ids}")
   522→                else:
   523→                    logger.warning(f"Reset {count} API orphaned tasks (IDs truncated)")
   524→            
   525→            return count
   526→            
   527→        except Exception as e:
   528→            logger.error(f"Failed to reset API worker orphaned tasks: {e}")
   529→            return 0
   530→    
   531→    async def reset_stale_assigned_tasks(self, timeout_minutes: int = 30) -> int:
   532→        """Reset tasks stuck in 'In Progress' that haven't been updated recently.
   533→        
   534→        This is a safety net that catches tasks where:
   535→        - The worker is alive and heartbeating
   536→        - But the task processing itself is stuck (deadlock, infinite loop, etc.)
   537→        - And updated_at isn't being refreshed
   538→        
   539→        Uses updated_at (last activity) rather than generation_started_at (task start time)
   540→        to catch tasks that made some progress but then stalled.
   541→        """
   542→        try:
   543→            cutoff_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(minutes=timeout_minutes)
   544→            
   545→            # Find tasks that are:
   546→            # - In Progress with a worker assigned (not api-worker-*, those are handled separately)
   547→            # - Haven't been updated in timeout_minutes
   548→            # - Have attempts < 3 (allow retry)
   549→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks').select('id, task_type, worker_id') \
   550→                .eq('status', 'In Progress') \
   551→                .not_.is_('worker_id', 'null') \
   552→                .not_.like('worker_id', 'api-worker-%') \
   553→                .lt('updated_at', cutoff_time.isoformat()) \
   554→                .lt('attempts', 3) \
   555→                .execute()
   556→            
   557→            if not result.data:
   558→                return 0
   559→            
   560→            # Filter out orchestrator tasks - they can run for extended periods
   561→            non_orchestrator_tasks = [
   562→                task for task in result.data 
   563→                if '_orchestrator' not in task.get('task_type', '').lower()
   564→            ]
   565→            
   566→            if not non_orchestrator_tasks:
   567→                logger.debug(f"Found {len(result.data)} stale tasks, but all are orchestrator tasks - skipping reset")
   568→                return 0
   569→            
   570→            task_ids = [task['id'] for task in non_orchestrator_tasks]
   571→            
   572→            # Reset these tasks back to Queued status
   573→            reset_result = self.supabase.table('tasks').update({
   574→                'status': 'Queued',
   575→                'worker_id': None,
   576→                'generation_started_at': None,
   577→                'generation_processed_at': None,
   578→                'error_message': f'Reset - no activity for {timeout_minutes} minutes (stale task)'
   579→            }).in_('id', task_ids).execute()
   580→            
   581→            count = len(reset_result.data) if reset_result.data else 0
   582→            
   583→            if count > 0:
   584→                logger.warning(f"Reset {count} stale assigned tasks with no activity for >{timeout_minutes}m")
   585→                if count <= 5:
   586→                    logger.warning(f"Reset stale task IDs: {task_ids}")
   587→                else:
   588→                    logger.warning(f"Reset {count} stale tasks (IDs truncated)")
   589→            
   590→            return count
   591→
   592→        except Exception as e:
   593→            logger.error(f"Failed to reset stale assigned tasks: {e}")
   594→            return 0
   595→
   596→    async def get_worker_task_failure_streak(
   597→        self, 
   598→        worker_id: str, 
   599→        window_minutes: int = 30
   600→    ) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   601→        """
   602→        Get recent task outcomes for a worker to detect failure streaks.
   603→        
   604→        Queries system_logs for "Finished task (Success: True/False)" messages
   605→        to determine if a worker is repeatedly failing tasks.
   606→        
   607→        Args:
   608→            worker_id: The worker ID to check
   609→            window_minutes: How far back to look for task outcomes
   610→            
   611→        Returns:
   612→            Dict with:
   613→                - consecutive_failures: Number of consecutive failures from most recent
   614→                - total_outcomes: Total task outcomes in window
   615→                - total_failures: Total failures in window
   616→                - failure_rate: failures / total (0.0 if no outcomes)
   617→        """
   618→        try:
   619→            cutoff_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(minutes=window_minutes)
   620→            
   621→            # Query system_logs for task completion messages from this worker
   622→            # "Finished task (Success: True)" or "Finished task (Success: False)"
   623→            result = self.supabase.table('system_logs') \
   624→                .select('message, timestamp') \
   625→                .eq('source_id', worker_id) \
   626→                .ilike('message', '%Finished task%') \
   627→                .gte('timestamp', cutoff_time.isoformat()) \
   628→                .order('timestamp', desc=True) \
   629→                .limit(50) \
   630→                .execute()
   631→            
   632→            if not result.data:
   633→                return {
   634→                    'consecutive_failures': 0,
   635→                    'total_outcomes': 0,
   636→                    'total_failures': 0,
   637→                    'failure_rate': 0.0
   638→                }
   639→            
   640→            # Parse outcomes (newest first)
   641→            outcomes = []
   642→            for log in result.data:
   643→                msg = log.get('message', '')
   644→                success = 'Success: True' in msg
   645→                outcomes.append(success)
   646→            
   647→            # Count consecutive failures from most recent
   648→            consecutive_failures = 0
   649→            for success in outcomes:
   650→                if not success:
   651→                    consecutive_failures += 1
   652→                else:
   653→                    break  # Hit a success, stop counting
   654→            
   655→            total_failures = sum(1 for s in outcomes if not s)
   656→            total_outcomes = len(outcomes)
   657→            
   658→            return {
   659→                'consecutive_failures': consecutive_failures,
   660→                'total_outcomes': total_outcomes,
   661→                'total_failures': total_failures,
   662→                'failure_rate': total_failures / total_outcomes if total_outcomes > 0 else 0.0
   663→            }
   664→            
   665→        except Exception as e:
   666→            logger.error(f"Failed to get task failure streak for worker {worker_id}: {e}")
   667→            # Return safe defaults on error (don't falsely flag worker)
   668→            return {
   669→                'consecutive_failures': 0,
   670→                'total_outcomes': 0,
   671→                'total_failures': 0,
   672→                'failure_rate': 0.0
   673→            }
   674→
   675→    # Batch operations for efficiency
   676→    async def batch_check_ever_claimed(self, worker_ids: List[str]) -> Dict[str, bool]:
   677→        """
   678→        Check if multiple workers have ever claimed tasks in one query.
   679→
   680→        Returns a dict mapping worker_id -> has_ever_claimed (bool).
   681→        Much more efficient than N separate queries.
   682→
   683→        NOTE: We don't use .limit() here because it could cause false negatives.
   684→        If worker A has 100 tasks, limit(N) might return all rows for A and miss B.
   685→        We deduplicate into a set anyway, so no limit is needed.
   686→        """
   687→        if not worker_ids:
   688→            return {}
   689→
   690→        try:
   691→            # Get worker_ids that have ever had a task assigned
   692→            # No limit - we deduplicate into a set, and the in_() filter
   693→            # ensures we only get rows for workers we care about
   694→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks') \
   695→                .select('worker_id') \
   696→                .in_('worker_id', worker_ids) \
   697→                .execute()
   698→
   699→            # Build set of workers that have claimed tasks (deduplication happens here)
   700→            claimed_workers = {r['worker_id'] for r in (result.data or []) if r.get('worker_id')}
   701→
   702→            # Return dict for all requested workers
   703→            return {wid: wid in claimed_workers for wid in worker_ids}
   704→
   705→        except Exception as e:
   706→            logger.error(f"Failed to batch check ever_claimed: {e}")
   707→            # Return True on error to avoid false terminations
   708→            return {wid: True for wid in worker_ids}
   709→
   710→    async def batch_check_active_tasks(self, worker_ids: List[str]) -> Dict[str, bool]:
   711→        """
   712→        Check if multiple workers have active (In Progress) tasks in one query.
   713→
   714→        Returns a dict mapping worker_id -> has_active_task (bool).
   715→        Much more efficient than N separate queries.
   716→
   717→        NOTE: No limit needed - "In Progress" tasks are naturally limited (one per worker
   718→        typically), and we deduplicate into a set anyway.
   719→        """
   720→        if not worker_ids:
   721→            return {}
   722→
   723→        try:
   724→            # Get worker_ids that have active tasks
   725→            result = self.supabase.table('tasks') \
   726→                .select('worker_id') \
   727→                .in_('worker_id', worker_ids) \
   728→                .eq('status', 'In Progress') \
   729→                .execute()
   730→
   731→            # Build set of workers with active tasks (deduplication happens here)
   732→            active_workers = {r['worker_id'] for r in (result.data or []) if r.get('worker_id')}
   733→
   734→            # Return dict for all requested workers
   735→            return {wid: wid in active_workers for wid in worker_ids}
   736→
   737→        except Exception as e:
   738→            logger.error(f"Failed to batch check active_tasks: {e}")
   739→            # Return False on error to be conservative
   740→            return {wid: False for wid in worker_ids}
   741→
```

```
    18→def _get_supabase_edge_urls() -> Dict[str, str]:

    45→async def count_tasks(client: httpx.AsyncClient, run_type: Optional[str]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    46→    """Count tasks using the new task-counts endpoint."""
    47→    try:
    48→        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL", "").rstrip("/")
    49→        task_counts_url = f"{supabase_url}/functions/v1/task-counts"
    50→        
    51→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] Counting tasks with run_type: {run_type}")
    52→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] URL: {task_counts_url}")
    53→        
    54→        if not supabase_url:
    55→            logger.error("[TASK_COUNT] SUPABASE_URL not configured")
    56→            return {"queued_plus_active": 0, "raw": None}
    57→        
    58→        headers = _auth_headers()
    59→        if not headers["Authorization"]:
    60→            logger.error("[TASK_COUNT] Missing required request context")
    61→            return {"queued_plus_active": 0, "raw": None}
    62→
    63→        payload: Dict[str, Any] = {"include_active": False}
    64→        if run_type:
    65→            payload["run_type"] = run_type
    66→
    67→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] Sending request with payload: {payload}")
    68→
    69→        resp = await client.post(task_counts_url, headers=headers, json=payload, timeout=15)
    70→        
    71→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] Response status: {resp.status_code}")
    72→        
    73→        if resp.status_code != 200:
    74→            logger.warning(f"[TASK_COUNT] Task counts failed with status {resp.status_code}")
    75→            logger.error(f"[TASK_COUNT] Error response: {resp.text}")
    76→            return {"queued_plus_active": 0, "raw": None}
    77→            
    78→        data = resp.json()
    79→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] Raw response data: {data}")
    80→        
    81→        # Extract the counts from the new endpoint format
    82→        # Assuming it returns the same structure as the old endpoint
    83→        if "totals" in data:
    84→            totals = data["totals"]
    85→            # For API workers, only consider queued tasks as claimable
    86→            available_count = int(totals.get("queued_only", totals.get("queued_plus_active", 0)))
    87→            logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] Using totals.queued_only: {available_count}")
    88→        else:
    89→            # Fallback: look for common count fields
    90→            available_count = int(data.get("queued_plus_active", data.get("available_tasks", 0)))
    91→            logger.debug(f"[TASK_COUNT] Using fallback count: {available_count}")
    92→        
    93→        logger.info(f"[TASK_COUNT] Available tasks: {available_count}")
    94→        return {"queued_plus_active": available_count, "raw": data}
    95→        
    96→    except (httpx.HTTPError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
    97→        logger.error(f"Count tasks failed: {exc}")
    98→        return {"queued_plus_active": 0, "raw": None}

   100→
   101→async def claim_next_task(client: httpx.AsyncClient, worker_id: str, run_type: Optional[str]) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   102→    """Claim the next available task for processing."""
   103→    urls = _get_supabase_edge_urls()
   104→    headers = _auth_headers()
   105→    
   106→    # Enhanced logging for debugging
   107→    logger.debug(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Worker {worker_id} attempting to claim task with run_type: {run_type}")
   108→    logger.debug(f"[TASK_CLAIM] URLs: {urls}")
   109→    
   110→    if not urls["claim"] or not headers["Authorization"]:
   111→        if not _has_logged_missing_env():
   112→            logger.error("[TASK_CLAIM] Missing endpoint configuration or request context; cannot claim tasks")
   113→            _set_logged_missing_env()
   114→        await asyncio.sleep(1)
   115→        return None
   116→
   117→    payload: Dict[str, Any] = {"worker_id": worker_id}
   118→    if run_type:
   119→        payload["run_type"] = run_type
   120→    
   121→    logger.debug(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Sending claim request with payload: {payload}")
   122→    
   123→    try:
   124→        resp = await client.post(urls["claim"], headers=headers, json=payload, timeout=15)
   125→        
   126→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Response status: {resp.status_code}")
   127→        
   128→        if resp.status_code == 204:
   129→            logger.info(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Worker {worker_id}: No tasks available to claim (204)")
   130→            return None
   131→            
   132→        resp.raise_for_status()
   133→        data = resp.json()
   134→        logger.info(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Worker {worker_id}: Successfully claimed task: {data.get('task_id')} ({data.get('task_type')})")
   135→        logger.debug(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Full task data: {data}")
   136→        
   137→        # No task type filtering - process any task with matching run_type
   138→        # The run_type filtering is handled by the edge function
   139→            
   140→        return data
   141→    except (httpx.HTTPError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
   142→        logger.error(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Worker {worker_id}: Claim failed with error: {exc}")
   143→        if isinstance(exc, httpx.HTTPStatusError):
   144→            logger.error(f"[TASK_CLAIM] Response body: {exc.response.text}")
   145→        await asyncio.sleep(0.5)
   146→        return None

   243→async def mark_complete(client: httpx.AsyncClient, task_id: str, result: Dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   244→    """Mark task complete - wrapper for backward compatibility"""

   262→async def update_task_metadata(task_id: str, metadata_updates: Dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   263→    """
   264→    Update task metadata (result_data field) to store tracking information.
   265→    
   266→    This is used to store fal.ai request IDs and other tracking data
   267→    that allows recovery if the orchestrator crashes mid-job.
   268→    
   269→    Args:
   270→        task_id: The task ID to update
   271→        metadata_updates: Dictionary of key-value pairs to merge into result_data
   272→        
   273→    Returns:
   274→        True if successful, False otherwise
   275→    """
   276→    try:
   277→        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
   278→        supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
   279→        
   280→        if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
   281→            logger.error("Missing SUPABASE_URL or SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY for metadata update")
   282→            return False
   283→        
   284→        client = create_client(supabase_url, supabase_key)
   285→        
   286→        # First, get the current result_data to merge with
   287→        result = client.table('tasks').select('result_data').eq('id', task_id).execute()
   288→        
   289→        if not result.data:
   290→            logger.error(f"Task {task_id} not found for metadata update")
   291→            return False
   292→        
   293→        current_data = result.data[0].get('result_data') or {}
   294→        
   295→        # Merge the new metadata
   296→        updated_data = {**current_data, **metadata_updates}
   297→        
   298→        # Update the task
   299→        update_result = client.table('tasks').update({
   300→            'result_data': updated_data
   301→        }).eq('id', task_id).execute()
   302→        
   303→        if update_result.data:
   304→            logger.info(f"Updated task {task_id} metadata with: {list(metadata_updates.keys())}")
   305→            return True
   306→        else:
   307→            logger.error(f"Failed to update task {task_id} metadata - no data returned")
   308→            return False
   309→            
   310→    except Exception as exc:
   311→        logger.exception(f"Failed to update task {task_id} metadata: {exc}")
   312→        return False
   313→
   314→
   315→async def get_task_metadata(task_id: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   316→    """
   317→    Get task metadata (result_data field) for recovery purposes.
   318→    
   319→    Args:
   320→        task_id: The task ID to query
   321→        
   322→    Returns:
   323→        The result_data dict if found, None otherwise
   324→    """
   325→    try:
   326→        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
   327→        supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
   328→        
   329→        if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
   330→            logger.error("Missing SUPABASE_URL or SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY for metadata query")
   331→            return None
   332→        
   333→        client = create_client(supabase_url, supabase_key)
   334→        
   335→        result = client.table('tasks').select('result_data').eq('id', task_id).execute()
   336→        
   337→        if result.data:
   338→            return result.data[0].get('result_data') or {}
   339→        else:
   340→            logger.warning(f"Task {task_id} not found for metadata query")
   341→            return None
   342→            
   343→    except Exception as exc:
   344→        logger.error(f"Failed to get task {task_id} metadata: {exc}")
   345→        return None
   346→
```

```
1→"""
     2→Database Log Handler for Orchestrator
     3→======================================
     4→
     5→Custom logging handler that batches logs and sends them to Supabase in the background.
     6→This allows centralized log storage without blocking the main orchestrator loop.
     7→
     8→Uses contextvars for proper async context isolation - each concurrent task maintains
     9→its own task_id, worker_id, and cycle_number without interference.
    10→
    11→Usage:
    12→    from database_log_handler import DatabaseLogHandler
    13→    from database import DatabaseClient
    14→    
    15→    db = DatabaseClient()
    16→    handler = DatabaseLogHandler(
    17→        supabase_client=db.supabase,
    18→        source_type="orchestrator_gpu",
    19→        source_id="orchestrator-main"
    20→    )
    21→    logging.getLogger().addHandler(handler)
    22→    
    23→    # Set current cycle for context (thread-safe, async-safe)
    24→    handler.set_current_cycle(5)
    25→"""
    26→
    27→import logging
    28→import queue
    29→import threading
    30→import time
    31→from contextvars import ContextVar
    32→from datetime import datetime, timezone
    33→from typing import Optional, Dict, Any
    34→from supabase import Client
    35→
    36→# Context variables for async-safe context tracking
    37→# These maintain separate values per async task/thread
    38→_context_cycle: ContextVar[Optional[int]] = ContextVar('cycle_number', default=None)
    39→_context_task_id: ContextVar[Optional[str]] = ContextVar('task_id', default=None)
    40→_context_worker_id: ContextVar[Optional[str]] = ContextVar('worker_id', default=None)
    41→
    42→
    43→class DatabaseLogHandler(logging.Handler):
    44→    """
    45→    Async logging handler that batches logs and sends to Supabase database.
    46→    
    47→    Runs a background thread that:
    48→    1. Collects log records into batches
    49→    2. Sends batches to database via RPC function
    50→    3. Prevents blocking the main application thread
    51→    
    52→    Features:
    53→    - Automatic batching for efficiency
    54→    - Background thread for non-blocking operation
    55→    - Graceful error handling (logs to stderr on failure)
    56→    - Context tracking (cycle number for orchestrator)
    57→    - Queue size limiting to prevent memory issues
    58→    """
    59→    
    60→    def __init__(
    61→        self,
    62→        supabase_client: Client,
    63→        source_type: str,
    64→        source_id: str,
    65→        batch_size: int = 50,
    66→        flush_interval: float = 5.0,
    67→        max_queue_size: int = 2000,
    68→        min_level: int = logging.INFO
    69→    ):
    70→        """
    71→        Initialize database log handler.
    72→        
    73→        Args:
    74→            supabase_client: Supabase client instance
    75→            source_type: Type of source ('orchestrator_gpu', 'orchestrator_api', 'worker')
    76→            source_id: Unique identifier for this source instance
    77→            batch_size: Number of logs to batch before sending (default: 50)
    78→            flush_interval: Seconds between automatic flushes (default: 5.0)
    79→            max_queue_size: Maximum queued logs before dropping (default: 2000)
    80→            min_level: Minimum log level to send to database (default: INFO)
    81→        """
    82→        super().__init__()
    83→        self.supabase = supabase_client
    84→        self.source_type = source_type
    85→        self.source_id = source_id
    86→        self.batch_size = batch_size
    87→        self.flush_interval = flush_interval
    88→        
    89→        # Only send logs at or above this level to database
    90→        self.setLevel(min_level)
    91→        
    92→        # Queue for batching logs
    93→        self.log_queue = queue.Queue(maxsize=max_queue_size)
    94→        
    95→        # Shutdown signal
    96→        self.shutdown_event = threading.Event()
    97→        
    98→        # Background thread for async sending
    99→        self.worker_thread = threading.Thread(
   100→            target=self._background_worker,
   101→            daemon=True,
   102→            name=f"DBLogHandler-{source_id}"
   103→        )
   104→        self.worker_thread.start()
   105→        
   106→        # Statistics
   107→        self.total_logs_queued = 0
   108→        self.total_logs_sent = 0
   109→        self.total_logs_dropped = 0
   110→        self.total_batches_sent = 0
   111→        self.total_errors = 0
   112→    
   113→    def set_current_cycle(self, cycle_number: Optional[int]):
   114→        """
   115→        Set current orchestrator cycle number for context.
   116→        Uses contextvars for async-safe context isolation.
   117→        """
   118→        _context_cycle.set(cycle_number)
   119→    
   120→    def set_current_task(self, task_id: Optional[str]):
   121→        """
   122→        Set current task ID for context.
   123→        Uses contextvars for async-safe context isolation - each concurrent task
   124→        maintains its own task_id without interference.
   125→        """
   126→        _context_task_id.set(task_id)
   127→    
   128→    def set_current_worker(self, worker_id: Optional[str]):
   129→        """
   130→        Set current worker ID for context.
   131→        Uses contextvars for async-safe context isolation.
   132→        """
   133→        _context_worker_id.set(worker_id)
   134→    
   135→    def emit(self, record: logging.LogRecord):
   136→        """
   137→        Queue log record for batch processing.
   138→        
   139→        This is called by the logging framework when a log is emitted.
   140→        We queue it for background processing to avoid blocking.
   141→        """
   142→        try:
   143→            # Build log entry
   144→            entry = {
   145→                'timestamp': datetime.fromtimestamp(record.created, tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
   146→                'source_type': self.source_type,
   147→                'source_id': self.source_id,
   148→                'log_level': record.levelname,
   149→                'message': self._format_message(record),
   150→                'metadata': {
   151→                    'module': record.module,
   152→                    'funcName': record.funcName,
   153→                    'lineno': record.lineno,
   154→                    'pathname': record.pathname,
   155→                    'thread': record.thread,
   156→                    'threadName': record.threadName,
   157→                }
   158→            }
   159→            
   160→            # Add context if available (from contextvars - async-safe)
   161→            cycle_number = _context_cycle.get()
   162→            if cycle_number is not None and cycle_number > 0:
   163→                entry['cycle_number'] = cycle_number
   164→            
   165→            task_id = _context_task_id.get()
   166→            if task_id:
   167→                entry['task_id'] = task_id
   168→            
   169→            worker_id = _context_worker_id.get()
   170→            if worker_id:
   171→                entry['worker_id'] = worker_id
   172→            
   173→            # Add exception info if present
   174→            if record.exc_info:
   175→                import traceback
   176→                entry['metadata']['exception'] = ''.join(
   177→                    traceback.format_exception(*record.exc_info)
   178→                )
   179→            
   180→            # Add extra fields from record
   181→            if hasattr(record, 'extra_data'):
   182→                entry['metadata']['extra'] = record.extra_data
   183→            
   184→            # Try to add to queue (non-blocking to avoid deadlock)
   185→            try:
   186→                self.log_queue.put_nowait(entry)
   187→                self.total_logs_queued += 1
   188→            except queue.Full:
   189→                # Queue is full, drop log to prevent memory issues
   190→                self.total_logs_dropped += 1
   191→                # Only log to stderr occasionally to avoid spam
   192→                if self.total_logs_dropped % 100 == 1:
   193→                    import sys
   194→                    print(
   195→                        f"WARNING: DatabaseLogHandler queue full, "
   196→                        f"dropped {self.total_logs_dropped} logs total",
   197→                        file=sys.stderr
   198→                    )
   199→                
   200→        except Exception:
   201→            # Don't let logging errors break the application
   202→            self.handleError(record)
   203→    
   204→    def _format_message(self, record: logging.LogRecord) -> str:
   205→        """Format log message, handling any formatting issues."""
   206→        try:
   207→            return record.getMessage()
   208→        except Exception:
   209→            # If message formatting fails, return raw message
   210→            return str(record.msg)
   211→    
   212→    def _background_worker(self):
   213→        """
   214→        Background thread that batches and sends logs to database.
   215→        
   216→        This runs continuously until shutdown, batching logs and
   217→        sending them via RPC function.
   218→        """
   219→        batch = []
   220→        last_flush_time = time.time()
   221→        
   222→        while not self.shutdown_event.is_set():
   223→            try:
   224→                # Calculate time until next flush
   225→                time_since_flush = time.time() - last_flush_time
   226→                timeout = max(0.1, self.flush_interval - time_since_flush)
   227→                
   228→                # Try to get log from queue with timeout
   229→                try:
   230→                    entry = self.log_queue.get(timeout=timeout)
   231→                    batch.append(entry)
   232→                except queue.Empty:
   233→                    pass
   234→                
   235→                # Flush if batch is full or interval elapsed
   236→                current_time = time.time()
   237→                should_flush = (
   238→                    len(batch) >= self.batch_size or
   239→                    (batch and current_time - last_flush_time >= self.flush_interval)
   240→                )
   241→                
   242→                if should_flush:
   243→                    self._flush_batch(batch)
   244→                    batch = []
   245→                    last_flush_time = current_time
   246→                    
   247→            except Exception as e:
   248→                # Log to stderr to avoid recursion
   249→                import sys
   250→                print(f"Error in DatabaseLogHandler background worker: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   251→                import traceback
   252→                traceback.print_exc(file=sys.stderr)
   253→                # Sleep briefly to avoid tight error loop
   254→                time.sleep(1)
   255→        
   256→        # Flush remaining logs on shutdown
   257→        if batch:
   258→            self._flush_batch(batch)
   259→    
   260→    def _flush_batch(self, batch: list):
   261→        """
   262→        Send batch of logs to database via RPC function.
   263→        
   264→        Args:
   265→            batch: List of log entry dictionaries
   266→        """
   267→        if not batch:
   268→            return
   269→        
   270→        try:
   271→            # Call RPC function to insert logs
   272→            result = self.supabase.rpc('func_insert_logs_batch', {
   273→                'logs': batch
   274→            }).execute()
   275→            
   276→            # Update statistics
   277→            self.total_batches_sent += 1
   278→            self.total_logs_sent += len(batch)
   279→            
   280→            # Log any errors from RPC function
   281→            if result.data:
   282→                errors = result.data.get('errors', 0)
   283→                if errors > 0:
   284→                    self.total_errors += errors
   285→                    import sys
   286→                    print(
   287→                        f"DatabaseLogHandler: {errors} logs failed to insert in batch",
   288→                        file=sys.stderr
   289→                    )
   290→            
   291→        except Exception as e:
   292→            # Log to stderr
   293→            self.total_errors += len(batch)
   294→            import sys
   295→            print(f"Failed to flush log batch of {len(batch)} entries: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
   296→            # Don't retry to avoid infinite loops
   297→    
   298→    def flush(self):
   299→        """
   300→        Force flush of any pending logs.
   301→        
   302→        This is synchronous and will block until the current queue is processed.
   303→        Use sparingly, primarily for testing or before shutdown.
   304→        """
   305→        # Give background thread time to process queue
   306→        max_wait = 10  # seconds
   307→        start_time = time.time()
   308→        
   309→        while not self.log_queue.empty() and time.time() - start_time < max_wait:
   310→            time.sleep(0.1)
   311→    
   312→    def close(self):
   313→        """
   314→        Shutdown handler gracefully.
   315→        
   316→        Signals background thread to stop and waits for it to finish.
   317→        Flushes any remaining logs before exiting.
   318→        """
   319→        # Signal shutdown
   320→        self.shutdown_event.set()
   321→        
   322→        # Wait for background thread to finish
   323→        if self.worker_thread.is_alive():
   324→            self.worker_thread.join(timeout=15)
   325→        
   326→        # Call parent close
   327→        super().close()
   328→    
   329→    def get_stats(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   330→        """
   331→        Get handler statistics.
   332→        
   333→        Returns:
   334→            Dictionary with statistics about log processing
   335→        """
   336→        return {
   337→            'source_type': self.source_type,
   338→            'source_id': self.source_id,
   339→            'total_logs_queued': self.total_logs_queued,
   340→            'total_logs_sent': self.total_logs_sent,
   341→            'total_logs_dropped': self.total_logs_dropped,
   342→            'total_batches_sent': self.total_batches_sent,
   343→            'total_errors': self.total_errors,
   344→            'queue_size': self.log_queue.qsize(),
   345→            'is_alive': self.worker_thread.is_alive()
   346→        }
   347→    
   348→    def __repr__(self):
   349→        return (
   350→            f"DatabaseLogHandler(source_type={self.source_type}, "
   351→            f"source_id={self.source_id}, "
   352→            f"sent={self.total_logs_sent}, "
   353→            f"dropped={self.total_logs_dropped})"
   354→        )
   355→
   356→
```

```
1→"""API Orchestrator main entry point.
     2→
     3→This module contains the event loop, task validation, and dispatch logic.
     4→Task handlers are implemented in task_handlers.py.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import os
     8→import asyncio
     9→import json
    10→import logging
    11→from typing import Any, Dict
    12→
    13→import httpx
    14→
    15→# Configure structured logging before importing internal modules
    16→from .logging_config import get_db_logging_stats, set_current_task, setup_logging
    17→from .database import DatabaseClient
    18→
    19→# Import utility modules
    20→from .task_utils import count_tasks, claim_next_task, mark_complete, mark_failed
    21→from .task_handlers import TASK_HANDLERS, SUPPORTED_TASK_TYPES
    22→
    23→RUN_TYPE = "api"  # Hardcoded for API workers - they process API tasks
    24→
    25→
    26→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27→
    28→
    29→def _load_runtime_environment() -> None:
    30→    """Load environment variables at runtime for CLI execution paths."""
    31→    from dotenv import load_dotenv
    32→
    33→    load_dotenv()
    34→
    35→
    36→async def process_api_task(task: Dict[str, Any], client: httpx.AsyncClient) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    37→    """Process API tasks by dispatching to the appropriate handler.
    38→
    39→    Args:
    40→        task: Task dictionary containing task_type and params
    41→        client: httpx client for making HTTP requests
    42→
    43→    Returns:
    44→        Result dictionary from the handler
    45→
    46→    Raises:
    47→        Exception: If task type is unsupported or handler fails
    48→    """
    49→    params = task.get("params") or {}
    50→    task_type = task.get("task_type", "unknown")
    51→
    52→    # Parse params if it's a JSON string
    53→    if isinstance(params, str):
    54→        try:
    55→            params = json.loads(params)
    56→        except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
    57→            logger.error(f"Failed to parse params JSON: {e}")
    58→            raise ValueError(f"Invalid JSON in params field: {e}") from e
    59→
    60→    # Check for wavespeed api_type override (legacy support)
    61→    if task_type == "qwen_image_edit" or params.get("api_type") == "wavespeed":
    62→        task_type = "qwen_image_edit"
    63→
    64→    # Look up the handler for this task type
    65→    handler = TASK_HANDLERS.get(task_type)
    66→
    67→    if handler is None:
    68→        raise ValueError(
    69→            f"Unsupported task type: {task_type}. "
    70→            f"Supported types: {', '.join(repr(t) for t in SUPPORTED_TASK_TYPES)}."
    71→        )
    72→
    73→    # Dispatch to the handler
    74→    return await handler(task, params, client)
    75→
    76→
    77→def validate_api_environment():
    78→    """Validate all required environment variables for API orchestrator."""
    79→    logger.info("🔍 [STARTUP] Validating API orchestrator environment...")
    80→
    81→    # Required environment variables
    82→    required_vars = {
    83→        'SUPABASE_URL': 'Supabase database URL for task management',
    84→        'SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY': 'Supabase service role key for database access',
    85→        'WAVESPEED_API_KEY': 'Wavespeed API key for processing tasks',
    86→        'FAL_KEY': 'fal.ai API key for image upscaling tasks',
    87→    }
    88→
    89→    # Optional but important environment variables
    90→    important_vars = {
    91→        'API_WORKER_ID': 'Unique identifier for this API worker instance',
    92→        'CONCURRENCY': 'Number of concurrent tasks to process',
    93→        'RUN_TYPE': 'Type of tasks to process (cloud/local)',
    94→        'PARENT_POLL_SEC': 'Polling interval for task checking',
    95→        'LOG_LEVEL': 'Logging level (DEBUG/INFO/WARNING/ERROR)',
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    # Check required variables
    99→    missing_required = []
   100→    for var, description in required_vars.items():
```

```
1→"""Task-oriented debug data services."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import Counter
     6→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
     7→import logging
     8→from typing import Any, Dict
     9→
    10→from scripts.debug.models import TaskInfo, TasksSummary
    11→
    12→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    13→
    14→
    15→def get_task_info(db: Any, log_client: Any, task_id: str) -> TaskInfo:
    16→    """Get complete task information: logs + DB state."""
    17→    logs = log_client.get_task_timeline(task_id)
    18→    result = db.supabase.table("tasks").select("*").eq("id", task_id).execute()
    19→    state = result.data[0] if result.data else None
    20→    return TaskInfo(task_id=task_id, state=state, logs=logs)
    21→
    22→
    23→def get_recent_tasks(
    24→    db: Any,
    25→    log_client: Any,
    26→    options: Dict[str, Any],
    27→) -> TasksSummary:
    28→    """Get recent tasks with analysis."""
    29→    limit = options.get("limit", 50)
    30→    status = options.get("status")
    31→    task_type = options.get("task_type")
    32→    worker_id = options.get("worker_id")
    33→    hours = options.get("hours")
    34→
    35→    query = db.supabase.table("tasks").select("*")
    36→    if status:
    37→        query = query.eq("status", status)
    38→    if task_type:
    39→        query = query.eq("task_type", task_type)
    40→    if worker_id:
    41→        query = query.eq("worker_id", worker_id)
    42→    if hours:
    43→        cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=hours)
    44→        query = query.gte("created_at", cutoff.isoformat())
    45→    tasks = query.order("created_at", desc=True).limit(limit).execute().data or []
    46→
    47→    status_dist = Counter(task.get("status") for task in tasks)
    48→    type_dist = Counter(task.get("task_type") for task in tasks)
    49→    processing_times = []
    50→    queue_times = []
    51→    for task in tasks:
    52→        if task.get("generation_started_at") and task.get("generation_processed_at"):
    53→            try:
    54→                started = datetime.fromisoformat(task["generation_started_at"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    55→                processed = datetime.fromisoformat(task["generation_processed_at"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    56→                processing_times.append((processed - started).total_seconds())
    57→            except (AttributeError, KeyError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
    58→                logger.debug("Skipping processing-time calculation for task %s: %s", task.get("id"), exc)
    59→                continue
    60→        if task.get("created_at") and task.get("generation_started_at"):
    61→            try:
    62→                created = datetime.fromisoformat(task["created_at"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    63→                started = datetime.fromisoformat(task["generation_started_at"].replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    64→                queue_times.append((started - created).total_seconds())
    65→            except (AttributeError, KeyError, TypeError, ValueError) as exc:
    66→                logger.debug("Skipping queue-time calculation for task %s: %s", task.get("id"), exc)
    67→                continue
    68→
    69→    timing_stats = {
    70→        "avg_processing_seconds": sum(processing_times) / len(processing_times) if processing_times else None,
    71→        "avg_queue_seconds": sum(queue_times) / len(queue_times) if queue_times else None,
    72→        "total_with_timing": len(processing_times),
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=hours or 24)
    76→    error_logs = log_client.get_logs(start_time=cutoff, log_level="ERROR", limit=100)
    77→    recent_failures = [
    78→        {
    79→            "task_id": log["task_id"],
    80→            "timestamp": log["timestamp"],
    81→            "message": log["message"],
    82→            "worker_id": log.get("worker_id"),
    83→        }
    84→        for log in error_logs
    85→        if log.get("task_id")
    86→    ]
    87→
    88→    return TasksSummary(
    89→        tasks=tasks,
    90→        total_count=len(tasks),
    91→        status_distribution=dict(status_dist),
    92→        task_type_distribution=dict(type_dist),
    93→        timing_stats=timing_stats,
    94→        recent_failures=recent_failures[:10],
    95→    )
    96→
    97→
    98→__all__ = ["get_recent_tasks", "get_task_info"]
    99→
```

```
1→"""Unified client for debug data access."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
     7→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
     8→
     9→from dotenv import load_dotenv
    10→from supabase import create_client
    11→
    12→from gpu_orchestrator.database import DatabaseClient
    13→from scripts.debug.models import OrchestratorStatus, SystemHealth, TaskInfo, TasksSummary, WorkerInfo, WorkersSummary
    14→from scripts.debug.services.system import get_orchestrator_status, get_system_health
    15→from scripts.debug.services.tasks import get_recent_tasks, get_task_info
    16→from scripts.debug.services.workers import check_worker_disk_space, check_worker_logging, get_worker_info, get_workers_summary
    17→
    18→
    19→class LogQueryClient:
    20→    """Client for querying system logs from Supabase."""
    21→
    22→    def __init__(self) -> None:
    23→        load_dotenv()
    24→        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
    25→        supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
    26→        if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
    27→            raise ValueError("SUPABASE_URL and SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY must be set in environment")
    28→        self.supabase = create_client(supabase_url, supabase_key)
    29→
    30→    def get_logs(
    31→        self,
    32→        start_time: datetime = None,
    33→        end_time: datetime = None,
    34→        source_type: str = None,
    35→        source_id: str = None,
    36→        worker_id: str = None,
    37→        task_id: str = None,
    38→        log_level: str = None,
    39→        cycle_number: int = None,
    40→        search_term: str = None,
    41→        limit: int = 1000,
    42→        order_desc: bool = True,
    43→    ) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    44→        """Query logs with flexible filters."""
    45→        if not start_time:
    46→            start_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=24)
    47→        if not end_time:
    48→            end_time = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    49→
    50→        query = self.supabase.table("system_logs").select("*")
    51→        query = query.gte("timestamp", start_time.isoformat()).lte("timestamp", end_time.isoformat())
    52→        if source_type:
    53→            query = query.eq("source_type", source_type)
    54→        if source_id:
    55→            query = query.eq("source_id", source_id)
    56→        if worker_id:
    57→            query = query.eq("worker_id", worker_id)
    58→        if task_id:
    59→            query = query.eq("task_id", task_id)
    60→        if log_level:
    61→            query = query.eq("log_level", log_level)
    62→        if cycle_number is not None:
    63→            query = query.eq("cycle_number", cycle_number)
    64→        if search_term:
    65→            query = query.ilike("message", f"%{search_term}%")
    66→
    67→        return query.order("timestamp", desc=order_desc).limit(limit).execute().data or []
    68→
    69→    def get_task_timeline(self, task_id: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    70→        """Get timeline of logs for a specific task."""
    71→        return self.get_logs(task_id=task_id, limit=1000, order_desc=False)
    72→
    73→
    74→class DebugClient:
    75→    """Unified client for all debugging data."""
    76→
    77→    def __init__(self) -> None:
    78→        self.log_client = LogQueryClient()
    79→        self.db = DatabaseClient()
    80→
    81→    def get_task_info(self, task_id: str) -> TaskInfo:
    82→        return get_task_info(self.db, self.log_client, task_id)
    83→
    84→    def get_worker_info(self, worker_id: str, hours: int = 24, startup: bool = False) -> WorkerInfo:
    85→        return get_worker_info(self.db, self.log_client, worker_id, hours=hours, startup=startup)
    86→
    87→    def check_worker_logging(self, worker_id: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    88→        return check_worker_logging(self.log_client, worker_id)
    89→
    90→    def check_worker_disk_space(self, worker_id: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    91→        return check_worker_disk_space(self.db, worker_id)
    92→
    93→    def get_recent_tasks(
    94→        self,
    95→        limit: int = 50,
    96→        status: Optional[str] = None,
    97→        task_type: Optional[str] = None,
    98→        worker_id: Optional[str] = None,
    99→        hours: Optional[int] = None,
   100→    ) -> TasksSummary:
   101→        return get_recent_tasks(
   102→            self.db,
   103→            self.log_client,
   104→            {
   105→                "limit": limit,
   106→                "status": status,
   107→                "task_type": task_type,
   108→                "worker_id": worker_id,
   109→                "hours": hours,
   110→            },
   111→        )
   112→
   113→    def get_workers_summary(self, hours: int = 2, detailed: bool = False) -> WorkersSummary:
   114→        _ = detailed
   115→        return get_workers_summary(self.db, hours=hours)
   116→
   117→    def get_system_health(self) -> SystemHealth:
   118→        return get_system_health(self.db, self.log_client)
   119→
   120→    def get_orchestrator_status(self, hours: int = 1) -> OrchestratorStatus:
   121→        return get_orchestrator_status(self.log_client, hours=hours)
   122→
```
